{'role': 'user', 'content': [{'type': 'text', 'text': '<file name="D:\\dev\\ab-tasks\\_briefs\\chessground\\brief.md">\n# Task: fix a reported bug in `chessground`\n\n## Bug report (from the project\'s issue tracker; reporter\'s proposed patch removed)\n**Disconnect ResizeObserver on destroy and on redraw**\n\n## Disconnect ResizeObserver on destroy and on redraw\n\n`bindBoard` constructs a `ResizeObserver` and observes `state.dom.elements.wrap`, but the observer is never stored or disconnected. This causes two distinct leaks:\n\n**Leak 1 — destroy:** `api.destroy()` calls `state.dom.unbind?.()`, which only unbinds the listeners registered by `bindDocument` (it returns the unbind function; `bindBoard` does not). The `ResizeObserver` is never disconnected, so the global RO controller continues to retain the wrap element — and through it, the entire detached board subtree.\n\n**Leak 2 — redraw:** `bindBoard` is called from inside `redrawAll`, which runs on every config change that rebuilds the DOM via `renderWrap`. Each call constructs a *new* RO observing the *new* wrap, but the *previous* RO is left observing the *previous* wrap (now detached). Within a single chessground lifetime, the observer count grows by one per full redraw. The previous RO\'s `onResize` callback also closes over the previous redraw\'s `elements`, so if it ever fires (e.g., the detached wrap is somehow resized), it operates on a partly-stale view of state.\n\n_(Reporter\'s proposed patch omitted.)_\n\n### Reproduction\n\n#### Reproduction — heap snapshot\n\n```html\n<!doctype html>\n<div id="root"></div>\n<script type="module">\nimport { Chessground } from \'@lichess-org/chessground\';\nwindow.test = () => {\n  for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {\n    const wrap = document.createElement(\'div\');\n    wrap.style.cssText = \'width:200px;height:200px\';\n    document.body.appendChild(wrap);\n    const cg = Chessground(wrap, { viewOnly: true });\n    cg.destroy();\n    wrap.remove();\n  }\n};\n</script>\n```\n\nRun `test()` from the console, take a heap snapshot, filter for `ResizeObserver` and `cg-board`. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Question: Are there other listeners or observers created in `bindBoard`/`bindDocument` that have the same lifecycle problem?\n\nLet me enumerate:\n\n**bindBoard** (per redraw, called every time `redrawAll` runs):\n1. `ResizeObserver` — already fixed.\n2. `contextmenu` listener on `boardEl` (conditional: disableContextMenu || drawable.enabled).\n3. `touchstart` listener on `boardEl` (non-viewOnly).\n4. `mousedown` listener on `boardEl` (non-viewOnly).\n\nThese listeners are attached to the `board` element. Question: is `board` (cg-board) recreated on each redraw? `renderWrap` executes `element.innerHTML = \'\'` (the wrap is cleared), and then creates a new `container` and a new `board`. So yes — a new `cg-board` element is created on every redraw. The listeners are attached to the old board element, and after the redraw that element is detached from the DOM.\n\nDo detached listeners leak? Listeners attached to a detached element do not retain anything beyond the element itself. Once the element is GC-eligible (nothing else references it), the element and its listeners get GC\'d together. The `onStart` closure closes over `s` (state) — but `s` is the shared `maybeState` object that lives for the entire lifetime of chessground anyway. So no additional retention beyond what state itself retains.\n\nThe critical difference from RO: the `ResizeObserver` instance is retained by the browser\'s global RO controller (a global strong reference to the callback → closure → the detached element subtree). Listeners have no global owner; the element is the owner, and the element is detached and unreferenced → GC-eligible. So listeners on the board element are not the same bug.\n\nBut wait — a subtle point: does anything else retain the old board element? `state.dom.elements` is replaced with new elements on each redraw, so the old `elements` object is only retained by: (a) the `onResize` closure of the old RO — fixed by the disconnect; (b) the old RO itself — fixed; (c) the old `onStart` closure? No — the closure lives on the element\'s listener list, and the element owns its listeners (element → listeners, one-way).
